Pawn on e7: if it moves, does it expose the king?
King is on d8. If e7 moves, the line d8-e7 is not a line for any white piece.
Wait, if Pawn e7 moves, the square e7 becomes empty.
Is there a white piece on e1-e7?
Column e: Row 1 ., Row 2 P, Row 3 ., Row 4 ., Row 5 ., Row 6 ., Row 7 ., Row 8 K.
No.
Pawn on a7: if it moves, is the king exposed?
King is on d8. No.
Pawn on g7: if it moves, is the king exposed?
No.
Pawn on d5: if it moves, is the king exposed?
Yes! If the pawn on d5 moves, the line d8-d5 is opened.
Is there a white piece on d1-d4?
Row 6 Col d is a white bishop ♗.
But bishops don't move vertically.
Wait, let's look at Column d again.
Row 1: ♚ (d8)
Row 2: ⭘ (d7)
Row 3: ⭘ (d6)
Row 4: ♟ (d5) - black pawn
Row 5: ♙ (d4) - white pawn
Row 6: ♗ (d3) - white bishop
Row 7: ⭘ (d2)
Row 8: ⭘ (d1)
Okay, nothing is pinning the pawns on a7, e7, g7.
This implies that the only reason they can't move is that Black is in check.

And the only way the King is in check is if someone can see d8.
Let's re-examine everything.
Queen a5: a5-b6-c7-d8.Blocked by Bishop c7.
Bishop c7: does not check d8.
Bishop d3: does not check d8.
Is it possible that the White Queen is not on a5?
Row 4: ♕ ⭘ ⭘ ♟ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘ ⭘
1:♕, 2:⭘, 3:⭘, 4:♟...
Wait, 1 is 'a'. So 'a5'.
Wait! I see it now!
Is the bishop on b6?
Row 2: ♟ ⭘ ♗ ⭘ ♟ ♝ ♟ ⭘
1:a7, 2:b7, 3:c7...
No, it's on c7.

Let me think about this from a different angle.
If the legal moves are d8e8, d8c8, d8d7, then d8 is under attack.
The attack must be coming from a piece that can't be blocked and doesn't allow other pieces to move.
If the king is in check, the only legal moves are those that remove the check.
If the only legal moves are the King's moves, then no other piece can remove the check.
Since we have pawns a7, e7, g7 and bishops f8, f7, none of which can block a check to d8 (they are not on the lines), the check must be coming from a piece and the King must move.
Who can check d8?
- A piece on the d-file (Rook, Queen). But we see the d-file is: K, ., ., P, P, B, ., . (No white Rook/Queen on d).
- A piece on the 8th rank (Rook, Queen). But Row 1 is: R, ., ., K, ., B, ., R. (No white Rook/Queen on the 8th rank).
- A piece on the diagonal.
Diagonals to d8:
1. c7, b6, a5.
Wait! a5 is the White Queen!
And c7 is the White Bishop!
If a white bishop is on c7, it blocks the queen on a5.
UNLESS... the piece on c7 is not a white bishop?
Wait, ♗ is white bishop. ♝ is black bishop.
Looking at Row 2: ♟ ⭘ ♗ ⭘ ♟ ♝ ♟ ⭘
Col 3 is ♗.
Wait! What if the white bishop is not on c7, but on b6?
Let me recount.
Row 2: ♟(a7) ⭘(b7) ♗(c7)
No, it's 3rd.
Wait, what if there is a White piece on d7?
Row 2: ♟ ⭘ ♗ ⭘ ♟ ♝ ♟ ⭘
4th is ⭘.

Wait a minute. a5, b6, c7, d8.
If the Queen is on a5 and the Bishop is on c7, they are both white.
Is it possible the check is coming from the Bishop on c7?
I said c7-d8 is not a diagonal.
Let's check: c7 is (3, 2), d8 is (4, 1).
Difference is (1, -1).
Wait! (3, 2) and (4, 1).
dx = 4-3 = 1.
dy = 1-2 = -1.
|dx| = |dy|.
IT IS A DIAGONAL!
The Bishop on c7 IS checking the King on d8!
My previous coordinate check was wrong.
c7 to d8 is a diagonal.
Let's verify:
a1 b1 c1 d1 e1 f1 g1 h1
a2 b2 c2 d2 e2 f2 g2 h2
...
a7 b7 c7 d7 e7 f7 g7 h7
a8 b8 c8 d8 e8 f8 g8 h8

Wait, the coordinates for c7: Column 3, Row 7 (from bottom).
The coordinates for d8: Column 4, Row 8 (from bottom).
Difference: Col +1, Row +1.
Yes, it is a diagonal.
So the White Bishop on c7 is checking the Black King on d8.
Now, can Black capture the Bishop on c7?
The King is on d8. Can it move to c7?
No, that would be Kd8xc7.
Is Kd8xc7 a legal move?
The legal moves are: d8e8, d8c8, d8d7.
Wait, d8xc7 is NOT in the list!
Why can't the King capture the bishop on c7?
Probably because the bishop on c7 is protected!
Who is protecting c7?
Look at the White Queen on a5.
a5 to c7:
a5 -> b6 -> c7.
It's a diagonal!
a5 is (1, 5), c7 is (3, 7).
Wait, a5 is Col 1, Row 5. c7 is Col 3, Row 7.
dx = 2, dy = 2.
It is a diagonal.
So the White Queen on a5 protects the White Bishop on c7.
Therefore, the Black King cannot capture the Bishop on c7.
Now, can any other Black piece capture c7?
Black pieces:
- K on d8
- B on f8, f7
- R on a8, h8
- P on a7, e7, g7, h6, d5
No Black piece can attack c7.
So, Black must move the King.
The legal moves are d8e8, d8c8, d8d7.
Let's evaluate these moves:
- d8e8: Moves the king to e8.
- d8c8: Moves the king to c8.
- d8d7: Moves the king to d7.

Which move is best?
If Kd8d7:
The king is now on d7.
Now current board has: King on d7, Bishop on c7, Queen on a5.
Is d7 attacked?
- Bishop on c7: d7 is adjacent. Not a diagonal.
- Queen on a5: a5 to d7? a5(1,5) to d7(4,7). dx=3, dy=2. No.
- White Bishop on d3: d3(4,3) to d7(4,7). dx=0, dy=4. No.
So d7 is safe.
If Kd8e8:
Is e8 attacked?
- Bishop on c7: c7(3,7) to e8(5,8). dx=2, dy=1. No.
- Queen on a5: a5(1,5) to e8(5,8). dx=4, dy=3. No.
- White Bishop on d3: d3(4,3) to e8(5,8). dx=1, dy=5. No.
So e8 is safe.
If Kd8c8:
Is c8 attacked?
- Bishop on c7: c7(3,7) to c8(3,8). dx=0, dy=1. No.
- Queen on a5: a5(1,5) to c8(3,8). dx=2, dy=3. No.
- White Bishop on d3: d3(4,3) to c8(3,8). dx=-1, dy=5. No.
So c8 is safe.

Comparison:
- d8d7: Moves the king forward. This might be aggressive or it might step into more checks.
- d8e8: Moves the king to the kingside.
- d8c8: Moves the king to the queenside.

Let's look at the other white pieces.
White has a bishop on d3 and a queen on a5.
If I move to d7, I'm closer to the center.
If I move to c8, I'm tucked away.
Actually, moving to d7 is often risky.
Let's look at the board state:
White: Qa5, Bc7, Bd3, Ke1, Ra1, Rh1, Nb1.
Black: Kd8, Bf8, Bf7, Ra8, Rh8, Pawns.
Black is in a very cramped position.
Kd8e8 seems like a standard move to get out of check and stay near the kingside pieces (Bf8, Rh8).
Wait, is d8d7 better?
If I play d8d7, I'm blocking the d-file, but that's already blocked by pawns.
Let's consider the move d8e8.
Wait, is there any reason to prefer one over the others?
Looking at the board:
The White Queen is at a5. The White Bishop is at c7.
If I move the king to e8, the king is further away from the white pieces.
If I move the king to c8, the king is closer to the white pieces.
If I move the king to d7, it's in the middle.
Given the lack of other pieces, e8 seems the safest.

Let's double check the moves.
Légal moves: d8e8, d8c8, d8d7.
I'll go with d8e8.
